Proposition 1 (Key-odds decomposition). For afixed surrounding context $z ,$ the query-dependent contribution to key selection is determined by the relative pretraining association between the query and the competing keys:

$$
{ \frac { P ( y _ { r } \mid z , x _ { q } ) } { P ( y _ { w } \mid z , x _ { q } ) } } \propto { \frac { P _ { \mathrm { p r e } } ( k _ { r } , x _ { q } ) } { P _ { \mathrm { p r e } } ( k _ { w } , x _ { q } ) } } = { \frac { \pi ( k _ { r } ) } { \pi ( k _ { w } ) } } { \frac { P _ { \mathrm { p r e } } ( x _ { q } \mid k _ { r } ) } { P _ { \mathrm { p r e } } ( x _ { q } \mid k _ { w } ) } } .\tag{2}
$$

1. Knowledge-deficit errors. Even under the correct key, the answer remains difuse, $P ( y _ { r } \mid z , x _ { q } , k _ { r } ) \approx P ( y _ { w } \mid$ $z , x _ { q } , k _ { r } )$ . The resulting variation across outputs or internal trajectories is most naturally captured by uncertainty- and <sub>represen</sub>t<sub>a</sub>ti<sub>on-</sub>b<sub>ase</sub>d d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>ors; re</sub>li<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence can a</sub>dditi<sub>ona</sub>ll<sub>y expose a m</sub>i<sub>ss</sub>i<sub>ng</sub> f<sub>ac</sub>t <sub>w</sub>h<sub>en one can</sub> b<sub>e</sub> <sub>supp</sub>li<sub>e</sub>d<sub>.</sub>

2. Wrong-knowledge errors. The model concentrates on an incorrect association or computation, $P ( y _ { w } \mid z , x _ { q } , k _ { r } )$ ≫ $P ( y _ { r } \mid z , x _ { q } , k _ { r } )$ <sub>.</sub> Si<sub>nce</sub> thi<sub>s</sub> <sub>error</sub> <sub>can</sub> <sub>rema</sub>i<sub>n</sub> <sub>con</sub>fid<sub>en</sub>t <sub>an</sub>d <sub>se</sub>lf<sub>-cons</sub>i<sub>s</sub>t<sub>en</sub>t<sub>,</sub> <sub>uncer</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>n</sub>t<sub>y</sub> i<sub>s</sub> <sub>wea</sub>k<sub>;</sub> <sub>compar</sub>i<sub>son</sub> <sub>w</sub>ith <sub>ex</sub>t<sub>erna</sub>l <sub>ev</sub>id<sub>ence</sub> i<sub>s</sub> th<sub>e mos</sub>t di<sub>rec</sub>t d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or.</sub>

Deterministic safe random-span controls. For each target, we require a non-target span with exactly the same tokenizer-token length. The eligible pool excludes every target span plus an eight-token guard band on either side, candidate-name and profile-value tokens, and relation, negation, or instruction words such as attended, awarded, never, choose, and answer. Control spans are selected without replacement, so the same span is never reused within a question. ## B.2.2 Pretraining Frequency–Dose Experiment

Classifier and evaluation protocol. We concatenate the scalar and hidden-state blocks and train a class-balanced lo<sub>g</sub>istic re<sub>g</sub>ression with � = 0.03, the liblinear solver, and at most 5,000 iterations. No model- or benchmark-s<sub>p</sub>ecific d<sub>e</sub>t<sub>ec</sub>t<sub>or</sub> h<sub>yperparame</sub>t<sub>ers are</sub> t<sub>une</sub>d f<sub>or</sub> th<sub>e ma</sub>t<sub>r</sub>i<sub>x repor</sub>t<sub>e</sub>d b<sub>e</sub>l<sub>ow.</sub> I<sub>n-</sub>d<sub>oma</sub>i<sub>n resu</sub>lt<sub>s use</sub> fi<sub>ve-</sub>f<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>ou</sub>t<sub>-o</sub>f<sub>-</sub>f<sub>o</sub>ld <sub>pre</sub>di<sub>c</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> repeated with split/PCA seeds 42, 43, and 44. ScientistQA folds are <sub>g</sub>rouped b<sub>y</sub> person, and GSM8K and DROP folds b<sub>y</sub> source item, usin<sub>g</sub> StratifiedGroupKFold. TriviaQA has one uni<sub>q</sub>ue source ke<sub>y p</sub>er item and therefore uses

StratifiedKFold. No <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub> a<sub>pp</sub>ears in both trainin<sub>g</sub> and evaluation within a <sub>g</sub>rou<sub>p</sub>ed s<sub>p</sub>lit. Comparison methods. The representation baselines in Table 4 are traced to three original methods. Aiersilan-style follows the mid-la<sub>y</sub>er hidden-state linear <sub>p</sub>robe of Aiersilan [2026]. [2025b]. The uncertaint<sub>y</sub> baselines are SelfCheckGPT’s sam<sub>p</sub>led-res<sub>p</sub>onse consistenc<sub>y</sub> score [Manakul et al., 2023], SeSE’s semantic structural entro<sub>py</sub> [Zhao et al., 2026], and Semantic Entro<sub>py</sub> over meanin<sub>g</sub>-e<sub>q</sub>uivalent <sub>g</sub>enerations [Far<sub>q</sub>uhar et al., 2024]. ## E.2 Prevalence Results

T<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> 26<sub>:</sub> E<sub>mp</sub>i<sub>r</sub>i<sub>ca</sub>l <sub>reg</sub>i<sub>me</sub> <sub>ass</sub>i<sub>gnmen</sub>t <sub>among</sub> <sub>or</sub>i<sub>g</sub>i<sub>na</sub>ll<sub>y</sub> i<sub>ncorrec</sub>t <sub>responses.</sub> C<sub>oun</sub>t<sub>s</sub> <sub>are</sub> f<sub>o</sub>ll<sub>owe</sub>d b<sub>y</sub> <sub>percen</sub>t<sub>ages</sub> within each benchmark. Scientist<sup>★</sup> denotes the subset of ScientistQA for which the relevant knowled<sub>g</sub>e is behaviorall<sub>y</sub> <sub>ver</sub>ifi<sub>e</sub>d <sub>as ava</sub>il<sub>a</sub>bl<sub>e</sub> t<sub>o</sub> th<sub>e mo</sub>d<sub>e</sub>l<sub>.</sub> Th<sub>e</sub> f<sub>our reg</sub>i<sub>mes are mu</sub>t<sub>ua</sub>ll<sub>y exc</sub>l<sub>us</sub>i<sub>ve.</sub>
<table><tr><td>Category</td><td>Scientist (n = 1334)</td><td> $\mathrm { { S c i e n t i s t } } ^ { \star }$   $( n = 4 5 3 )$ </td><td>TriviaQA (n = 500)</td><td>GSM8K (n = 471)</td><td>DROP (n = 500)</td></tr><tr><td>Wrong knowledge</td><td>72 (5.40%)</td><td>0 (0.00%)</td><td>238 (47.60%)</td><td>34 (7.20%)</td><td>43 (8.60%)</td></tr><tr><td>Knowledge deficit</td><td>573 (42.95%)</td><td>66 (14.57%)</td><td>187 (37.40%)</td><td>99 (21.00%)</td><td>120 (24.00%)</td></tr><tr><td>Context distract</td><td>463 (34.71%)</td><td>258 (56.95%)</td><td>56 (11.20%)</td><td>222 (47.10%)</td><td>285 (57.00%)</td></tr><tr><td>Unstable inference</td><td>204(15.29%)</td><td>118 (26.05%)</td><td>12 (2.40%)</td><td>94 (20.00%)</td><td>49 (9.80%)</td></tr><tr><td>Unclassified</td><td>22 (1.65%)</td><td>11 (2.43%)</td><td>7 (1.40%)</td><td>22 (4.70%)</td><td>3 (0.60%)</td></tr></table>

Interpretation. The within-benchmark shifts, rather than a literal cross-benchmark comparison of percentages, provide the cleanest evidence. Restrictin<sub>g</sub> ScientistQA to the le<sub>g</sub>ac<sub>y</sub> probe-known cohort reduces knowled<sub>g</sub>e deficit from 42.95% t<sub>o</sub> 14<sub>.</sub>57% <sub>an</sub>d i<sub>ncreases con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> f<sub>rom</sub> 34<sub>.</sub>71% t<sub>o</sub> 56<sub>.</sub>95%<sub>.</sub> GSM8K <sub>con</sub>t<sub>a</sub>i<sub>ns</sub> b<sub>o</sub>th <sub>a</sub> l<sub>arge con</sub>t<sub>ex</sub>t<sub>-</sub>di<sub>s</sub>t<sub>rac</sub>ti<sub>on</sub> com<sub>p</sub>onent (47.10%) and a combined 41.00% knowled<sub>g</sub>e-deficit/unstable com<sub>p</sub>onent, which motivates the fusion experiments below. TriviaQA is instead dominated b<sub>y</sub> failed closed-book probes (85.00% across wron<sub>g</sub> knowled<sub>g</sub>e and knowled<sub>g</sub>e deficit).

These are behavioral si<sub>g</sub>natures rather than identified latent causes.
